common.help.favourite.what = Right-click a quote (long-press on a phone) and choose Favourite, next to Edit and Delete.
common.help.favourite.more = The ♥ on the card does the same but only shows on hover, so on a phone use the menu. It reads Unfavourite when the quote is already a favourite.

# The right-click menu on a book, film or show cover.
common.help.cover-menu.term = A cover’s own menu
common.help.cover-menu.what = Right-click a book, film or show (long-press on a phone) for Select, Fill gaps, quiz, Edit and Delete.
common.help.cover-menu.more = The same actions as the selection bar, for one item. Delete asks first, says how many quotes go with it, and offers Undo.

# The selection-bar toggle that takes things out of the Daily Quiz.
common.help.skip-in-quiz.term = Skip in quiz
common.help.skip-in-quiz.what = For things you keep but don't want to be quizzed on — a list saved as a quote, a manual full of page numbers.
common.help.skip-in-quiz.more = Select them and press Skip in quiz; nothing is deleted. On a book, it also covers highlights you add later. The button reads Add to quiz when they're already skipped.

# The selection-bar action that fetches only the EMPTY fields.
common.help.fill-gaps.term = Fill gaps
common.help.fill-gaps.what = For selected books, films or shows: fetches metadata and fills only the empty fields.
common.help.fill-gaps.more = Anything you've filled in is left alone, so there's no preview. To review every difference, use Re-verify in Metadata.

# The circled i beside a control.

common.help.info-dots.what = The small circled “i” next to a control explains it.
common.help.info-dots.more = On desktop, hover to open it or click to keep it open. On a phone, tap.

# What holding a finger down does.
common.help.long-press.term = Long press
common.help.long-press.what = What it does depends on what is under your thumb.
common.help.long-press.more = On a shelf card, it selects the card; where there is nothing to select, it opens the card's menu. On a control, it shows the control's name without pressing it. On quote text, it lets your phone select the text.

# --- Pointer devices only — the tab strip, hover, the keyboard ---

# The shortcuts, and the sheet ? opens.
common.help.keyboard.term = Keyboard
common.help.keyboard.what = Press ? for the full list. / searches, N captures a quote, and G then H, L, C, Q or S goes to Home, Library, Catalogue, Quotes or Stats.
common.help.keyboard.more = In a quiz, 1 and 2 grade and Space turns a flip card. Each shortcut is also shown on its button.

# The always-visible desktop tab strip that stands in for the drawer.
common.help.tab-strip.term = Tab strip
common.help.tab-strip.what = Every screen, always in the top bar: the ones you use, then the tools.
common.help.tab-strip.more = Which screens appear is set in Settings. When space is short it shows icons only, and each names itself on hover.

# The bubble a glyph-only control shows on hover.
common.help.hover-labels.term = Hover labels
common.help.hover-labels.what = Every icon-only control shows its name in a small bubble when you hover or tab to it.

# The right-click menu on a quote card.
common.help.card-menu.term = Right-click a card
common.help.card-menu.what = Right-click a quote card for copy, share, edit and delete, opened where you clicked.
common.help.card-menu.more = Shift+F10 or the Menu key opens it from the keyboard; Escape closes it. If you've selected text in the card, the browser's own menu opens instead.

# The capture surface's per-kind locator boxes (1.17.0). A game is placed by its
# act and its quest and has no timestamp at all — the server clears one on a game's
# line — so the form asks for the pair the medium actually has. The episode's name
# is the same locator as its number, said in words.
capture.form.act.placeholder = e.g. Act II
capture.form.quest.placeholder = e.g. The Battle of Kaer Morhen
capture.form.episode-name.placeholder = e.g. The Rains of Castamere
# The actor the chosen character implies, from the work's own cast. Read-only: the
# server derives the stored actor, so this says the character matched a real row.
capture.form.played-by.prose = played by {name}

# The one on-demand IMDb pass (1.17.0) — a cast for the works whose structured

# title it attached because that is the only check against exactly that.

# ---- answering a finding (2.2.1) -------------------------------------------
# The page reported and fixed nothing; it now offers the rewrite and remembers a
# refusal. The copy's job is to make accepting safe: the reader must know what will
# change before they press, and know that ignoring changes nothing at all.
cleanup.bucket.heading = show
cleanup.bucket.open.label = To answer
cleanup.bucket.ignored.label = Ignored ({n})
cleanup.rescan.label = Look again
cleanup.accept.label = Accept
cleanup.ignore.label = Ignore
cleanup.ignore.tip = Stop offering this one. The quote is not changed.
cleanup.restore.label = Offer it again
cleanup.select-all.label = select all {n}
cleanup.row.pick.aria = Pick this {rule} find
cleanup.bulk.accept.label = Accept {n}
cleanup.bulk.ignore.label = Ignore these
cleanup.bulk.restore.label = Offer these again
cleanup.state.none-ignored = Nothing ignored yet. A finding you ignore waits here, and stops being offered.
cleanup.toast.applied = {n} corrected
cleanup.toast.stale = {n} had already changed, so nothing was done to them
cleanup.toast.duplicate = {n} would have matched a quote you already keep, so they were left alone
cleanup.toast.ignored = {n} ignored
cleanup.toast.restored = {n} back on the list

# A game's line, in the form it is corrected in (2.2.1). A game has no runtime, so
# where a film's line has a timestamp a game's has an act and a quest — the two the
# dedupe hash is keyed by, since a bark reused in two quests is two quotes.
film.line.form.act.placeholder = act
film.line.form.act.tip = Which act or chapter this line is in — free text, so "Prologue" is an answer.
film.line.form.quest.placeholder = quest
film.line.form.quest.tip = The quest or mission this line is from. The same line can be saved under two quests.

# What a kind of standalone quote carries (0047's five, on screen at last in 2.2.1).
# Grouped under one heading because the kind lives on the board and not on the quote,
# so the form cannot know which of them applies — and because the alternative,
# boxes appearing and disappearing under a Select, hides a field somebody has filled.

quotes.form.recipient.placeholder = who it was written to
quotes.form.work-title.placeholder = the essay or article it is from

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# identity.* — the person panel and the character page (0056). THE THREE SCOPES
# ARE THE SCHEMA, and each section's second line is what stops a reader
# believing they renamed an author on thirty-one other books. Those lines are
# not decoration and must not be trimmed to fit.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

identity.character.title = Character
identity.person.saved = Saved to the record
identity.character.saved = Saved to the record

# Scope 1: the credit link, and nothing else.
identity.scope.work.title = on this work
identity.credit.as.on = as {as}

# Scope 2: what the record is in, and what finds it.

identity.scope.library.character = Every work this character appears in, and every spelling that finds them.

identity.character.appearances.title.other = in {n} works

# The five character/people screens (task 16), in the design pack's own order.
# `identity.crumb.*` is the header's second line; `identity.section.*` its
# headings; `identity.row.*` the rows under them. The two `.tip` keys on
# identity.works.add name what a work is added TO, which is the one thing that
# differs between the two tables: a character appears IN a work, a person is
# credited ON one.
identity.crumb.book.one = {n} book
identity.crumb.book.other = {n} books
identity.crumb.film.one = {n} film
identity.crumb.film.other = {n} films
identity.crumb.show.one = {n} show
identity.crumb.show.other = {n} shows
identity.crumb.game.one = {n} game
identity.crumb.game.other = {n} games
identity.crumb.works.one = {n} work
identity.crumb.works.other = {n} works
identity.crumb.badge.book = book
identity.crumb.badge.film = film
identity.crumb.badge.show = show
identity.crumb.badge.game = game
# THE PICTURE'S REAL SIZE, measured off the file rather than described. The pack
# prints "266 × 350 px" under every portrait, because a picture field cannot be
# judged from a thumbnail: a reader who uploads a 266-wide portrait finds out it
# was too small when a share card goes out soft, which is too late.
identity.portrait.px = {w}×{h}px
identity.portrait.zoom.aria = See {name}’s picture full screen
# THE SIZE FLOOR, SAID AS A SIZE. This warning used to be spelled "low contrast"
# while the code that showed it tested `w < 400 || h < 400` — so a small picture
# was labelled low contrast and a washed-out one said nothing. The owner spotted
# it from the screen. Two facts, two strings.
identity.portrait.small = under {n}px
# AND THE CONTRAST, NOW MEASURED: luminance over a downsample, the spread between
# the 5th and 95th percentiles. Shown only where it can be measured — a remote
# file taints the canvas, and a guess about somebody's portrait is worse than
# silence.
identity.portrait.soft = low contrast
# THE SHAPE, only where it is not the 2:3 the app draws portraits at. Everything
# else is centre-cropped by `object-fit: cover`, silently — so half a face can be
# outside the circle with nothing on the screen to say so.
identity.portrait.ratio = {a}:{b}, cropped
# The fallback where nothing has been measured yet — no picture, or one still
# loading. Never printed beside a size.
identity.portrait.global = the record's own picture

# ---- THE FOUR WAYS A PICTURE ARRIVES -----------------------------------------
# The design pack names three and calls them "the repo's order" — ask the
# suppliers, take a file from this machine, paste an address — and adds a fourth
# on a work's own sheet that is the only one that leaves the work. They are NAMED
# buttons rather than icons because a picture verb that goes wrong is expensive to
# undo and an unlabelled glyph is a guess.
identity.picture.fetch.label = Fetch
identity.picture.fetch.tip = Look for a better one at the sources
identity.picture.upload.label = Upload
identity.picture.upload.tip = A file from this machine
identity.picture.upload.aria = Choose a picture file for {name}
identity.picture.paste.label = Paste URL
identity.picture.paste.tip = From the web, by address
# The fourth, and the reason it is drawn dashed and in the danger colour: it is
# the one press on these screens whose effect is not confined to the work being
# looked at.
identity.picture.promote.label = Set for the identity
identity.picture.promote.tip = Use this everywhere
identity.picture.promote.ask.title = Use this picture for the identity?
identity.picture.promote.ask.body = It becomes the character's picture everywhere a work has not set its own. This work keeps its own picture either way.
identity.picture.promote.ask.verb = Yes, set it everywhere
# ---- WHOSE PICTURE IS ACTUALLY ON THE SCREEN ---------------------------------
# A local sheet draws THIS work's picture of the character when it has one and
# falls back the way every chip in the app does — the identity's own, then the
# performer's headshot. A fallback that does not say so is a screen claiming the
# work holds a picture it does not, which is the one thing store.CastOf's own
# comment forbids: "a panel that shows the global picture where the work has none
# must be able to SAY so".
identity.portrait.from.identity = the character's own picture
identity.portrait.from.actor = {name}'s photograph
identity.portrait.from.none = no picture for the role
# ---- the pack's LOCAL character sheets (char-book, char-film, char-game) ----
# One character seen from inside one work. Everything here is true of THIS work
# and of no other; what is true of the identity lives on the global sheet, one
# door away.

# The crumb under the name. Never "Harry Potter · Deathly Hallows" — the sheet is
# about a character IN a work, and the preposition is the whole of that fact.
identity.crumb.in = in {title}
# Shown under the portrait until the file has been measured. See identity.portrait.px.
identity.portrait.local = this work's own picture

# WHAT THE WORK CALLS THEM, and the label differs by medium on purpose: a novel
# NAMES a character in its text, a film CREDITS a performance. The reader editing
# one is not editing the other kind of thing.
identity.row.called.label = Called here
identity.row.credited.label = Credited as
# The sub-line when the work has no second name for them. Not "none": the row's
# value is the name, which is present — what is absent is any OTHER name.

# THE THREE FACTS UNDER THE NAME. A game draws two of them: "age here" is a fact
# about a performance, and a playable character has no single one.
identity.facts.part = Part
identity.facts.first = First appears
identity.facts.age = Age here
identity.facts.none = not set

# The reader's own note on ONE casting. The sub-line has to say "this work only",
# because the record's own description is the field that reaches every work and
# the two look alike in a list.
identity.row.note.for = Note on {name}’s credit

# THE COUNTS, WHICH ARE DOORS INTO SEARCH. Pressing one lands on search with this
# character and this work already up as chips, and the second adds `favourite:Yes`
# so the narrower figure opens the narrower search.
# AND THE SCOPE IS PER SHEET WHERE THE CAPTION IS NOT. The same two words —
# quotes, favourited — on all four sheets, because that is what makes the numbers
# comparable; the TOOLTIP says what each is counted over, because a figure whose
# scope is ambiguous is a figure the reader cannot check. A person's pair has no
# door: their lines are linked as a performer OR as a quoted speaker, two facets
# that AND together, so one press could only ever reach half of what was counted.
identity.count.favourites.tip.character = Search this character’s favourited lines, in every work
identity.count.favourites.tip.person = Every favourited line this person has said
identity.count.favourites.tip.work = Search this work’s favourited lines
identity.count.quotes.tip.character = Search this character’s lines, in every work
identity.count.quotes.tip.person = Every line this person has said
identity.count.quotes.tip.work = Search this work’s lines
identity.count.quotes.one = quote
identity.count.quotes.other = quotes
identity.count.favourites.one = favourited
identity.count.favourites.other = favourited
identity.count.favourites.tip = Search this character’s favourited lines in this work
identity.count.quotes.tip = Search this character's lines in this work

# THE WAY UP, to the character out of any one work.
identity.row.global.label = Open the global record
identity.row.global.sub = Across every work

# --- giving a work-level character a SECOND work (the owner's item 3). The label
# names the door; the sub names the consequence, which is the half the owner
# asked about — "that will get added to the global-character (which should in
# turn enable global character for the character as well)". It is one credit
# either way: the global screen is a consequence of the count, not a flag.
identity.row.add-work.label = Also in another work
identity.row.add-work.sub = One character in both, with a screen of its own
identity.badge.global = global

# AND THE WAY OUT, which is not a delete — hence a whole section rather than a
# key beside the others. A book's reassurance is shorter than a film's because a
# book has nobody playing anybody: there are no people's records to speak for.
identity.section.remove.label = Remove
identity.row.unlink.label.book = Remove from this book
identity.row.unlink.label.film = Remove from this film
identity.row.unlink.label.show = Remove from this show
identity.row.unlink.label.game = Remove from this game
identity.row.unlink.sub.book = Other works keep the character
identity.row.unlink.sub.cast = Other works keep the character
# ---- the picker, which is how every row above is changed --------------------
# THE PACK'S `openPicker`, and the reason it is one sheet rather than nine. A row
# on an identity screen states its value and opens this to change it; the sheet
# asks one question, wears the app's ✓ / ✕ pair, and counts what the press will
# write. What it replaces is a block of plain inputs behind one "Save" button,
# which is neither half of that pair.
identity.picker.blocked = Type something first
identity.picker.save.tip = Writes this one field
# ---- the person mode, which is a LIST and not a box -------------------------
# The pack's caption for it is "Everyone the app already knows", and the reason
# it matters is that ResolvePerson folds on the name: typing a name that is
# nearly one you have makes a SECOND record, with its own portrait fetch and its
# own page, and you find out when a face is missing from a credit you were sure
# you filled in.
identity.picker.person.label = Who
identity.picker.person.placeholder = Search or type a name
identity.picker.person.new = “{name}” is new — saving adds them
identity.picker.lang.other = Or type a language
# THE SAME FIVE IN BOTH LOCALES, on purpose. A dubbing credit names the language
# it is IN, so "বাংলা" is what a Bengali dub is called on an English screen too —
# translating these would make one credit read differently depending on who was
# looking at it. They live here rather than in the component because that is
# where copy lives (no-hardcoded-bengali.test.js), not because they vary.
identity.picker.lang.suggestions = English · हिन्दी · বাংলা · 日本語 · Français
# ONE FIELD FOR THE NAME AND EVERY OTHER NAME — the pack's ruling, and its
# reasoning: two rows editing one fact made the printing name look like a
# different KIND of thing from its spellings, when it is only the first of them.
identity.local.names.hint = One name per line. The first is shown on quotes and credits; the rest are alternative spellings search will find.
identity.local.names.placeholder = The name that prints\nAnother spelling
# ---- WHAT THIS WORK SAYS ABOUT THEM ------------------------------------------
# `work_cast.description`, the per-work half of a pair whose other half is the
# record's own. The sub-line states the scope for the same reason the note's does:
# the two fields look alike and reach very different distances.
identity.row.local-desc.label = Who they are here
identity.row.local-desc.sub = This work only
identity.row.local-desc.none = none
identity.local.names.saved = Names saved — the first line prints
identity.credit.add.blocked = Name the performer first
# ---- the performer block, on char-film and char-game -----------------------
# THE HEADING IS THE CONTROL, because there are exactly two answers. A film's
# cast is played and a game's is voiced — usually. An animated feature is a film
# whose cast is voiced and a medium cannot know that, so movies.cast_role holds
# the work's own answer and this pair sets it.
identity.section.actorby = Played by
identity.section.voiceby = Voiced by
identity.seg.played = Played by
identity.seg.voiced = Voiced by
# A DUB IS A SECOND CAST IN ANOTHER LANGUAGE. Film and show only: a game's
# localisation is its voice cast rather than a layer over it, so its languages
# ride on the voice credits themselves.
identity.section.dubbedby = Dubbed by
# A CREDIT WITH NOBODY IN IT IS A REAL STATE, not a blank to be hidden: a film
# credits a de-aged shot or a second voice the reader has not named, and hiding
# the row would lose the note beside it — the only record the casting exists.
identity.credit.unnamed = Not named yet
identity.credit.no-performer = no performer
identity.credit.add.dub.lang.label = Dubbed in which language
identity.credit.add.save.label = Add the credit

identity.credit.open.tip = Open their record
# The pack's own words for a credit nobody is named on. It is a real state — a

# ---- add a link from a provider id ----------------------------------------
# "Add a link" used to focus the free-text field, so a reader holding a person's
# IMDb id had to know the page is /name/<id>/ — not /person/ or /people/ —
# before they could type anything. Every pattern the popup writes is the one
# internal/metadata/people.go already builds when it fetches a portrait, so a
# link typed by hand and a link fetched from TMDB are the same string.
# NOT "FROM AN ID" ANY MORE. The sheet opens on the custom row, where an ordinary
# address is pasted, so naming it after the id space would name the case it is
# NOT in by default.
identity.link.id.title = Add a link
identity.link.id.provider.label = Which site
identity.link.id.field.label = The ID
# The custom row asks for an address, not an id, and a field labelled "The ID"
# over a box you paste a URL into is the wrong noun.
identity.link.id.field.label.url = The address
identity.link.id.provider.custom = Any address
identity.link.id.save.tip = Add this link
# Shown on the ✓ while there is nothing to write. Inside the five-word rule.
identity.link.id.save.blocked = Type an ID first
identity.link.id.save.blocked.url = Paste an address first
# Shown on the ✓ when something IS typed and it is not an id of the chosen space —
# the enforcement, said before the press rather than discovered by pressing a dead
# pill later.
identity.link.id.save.wrong = Not a {site} ID
identity.link.id.example.label = For example
# THE HINT IS THE RULE and the example is how to get there from a page you are
# looking at. Both, because the rule alone leaves a reader holding a full address
# with nothing telling them which part of it to keep.
identity.link.id.hint.custom = Any page on the web. Paste one of the sites below and it files itself.
identity.link.id.hint.imdb = nm and then seven or eight digits
identity.link.id.hint.tmdb = Digits only
identity.link.id.hint.tvdb = The number, or the name as the address spells it

identity.link.id.hint.igdb = The name as the address spells it
identity.link.id.example.custom = en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajesh_Khanna
identity.link.id.example.imdb = imdb.com/name/nm0000123/ → nm0000123
identity.link.id.example.tmdb = themoviedb.org/person/10859-ryan-reynolds → 10859
identity.link.id.example.tvdb = thetvdb.com/people/rajesh-khanna → rajesh-khanna
identity.link.id.example.amazon = amazon.com/stores/author/B001H6TVXK → B001H6TVXK

# ---- THE CHOOSE SHEET, for a press with more than one right answer -----------
# A tile stands for a work, and there is more than one thing behind a work: the
# work itself, this character as that work has them, and whoever played them.
# The pack is emphatic that guessing is not an option here — picking wrong opens
# the wrong record, and the reader then edits it.
identity.choose.work.hint = There is more than one thing here. Which do you want?
identity.choose.work.sub = the work's own page
identity.choose.work.unreachable = This screen has no way back to the work
identity.choose.local.sub = this character, as this work has them
identity.choose.actor.sub = the performer's own record
# THE THIRD DESTINATION BEHIND A SPEAKER CHIP, offered ONLY where the identity
# holds more than one work — with one work the local record IS the identity, and
# a door to it would lead back to the screen you are standing on.
identity.choose.global.sub = the character, across every work
# And the same question asked the other way round: one performer, two roles in one
# work. Only asked where there IS a second role — one role behind a tile opens.
identity.choose.roles.hint = They have more than one role in this. Which do you want?
identity.choose.role.sub = the character, as this work has them
# ---- REMOVING THE IDENTITY FROM EVERY WORK -----------------------------------
# Not a bulk verb, deliberately: the reach is stated and then the list is handed
# back, so each work is unlinked by its own press. Slower on purpose, and the
# slowness is the safety — this is the one act on the screen whose damage could
# not be seen before it happened.
identity.remove-all.ask.title = This removes the character from every work
identity.remove-all.ask.body = There is no bulk unlink: unlink each work yourself. Each work keeps its record, quotes and pictures.
identity.remove-all.unlink.sub = unlink this work
identity.row.delete.label.person = Delete this person
identity.row.delete.label.company = Delete this company
identity.row.delete.sub.person = The credits go; the works and the characters stay
identity.row.delete.sub.company = The credits go; the works stay
identity.works.add.character.tip = Add a work this character appears in
identity.works.add.person.tip = Add a work this person is credited on
identity.works.add.company.tip = Add a work this company is credited on

# ---- a bin entry's works, and the face an identity entry took ------------
# One row per book or film inside an entry — a bulk delete is one entry, and the
# title with its own count is the only thing in it a reader can recognise.
